Abstract: Energy reconstruction from electromagnetic calorimeter data underpins particle identification, event reconstruction and precision measurements in high-energy physics. Machine learning already performs well on this task, yet two questions remain open: how much the choice of data representation matters, and whether hybrid quantum-classical models add value in practice. We compare classical and quantum-enhanced models for incident positron energy reconstruction on the CaloGAN dataset, a public benchmark of 100,000 simulated electromagnetic shower events spanning 1 to 100 GeV. Eight architectures, namely SVR, ANN, CNN and LSTM together with their quantum counterparts, are trained on three calorimeter representations: engineered physical features, calorimeter images, and longitudinal shower sequences. Models that follow the longitudinal development of the shower reach the highest accuracy, which points to the value of physically informed representations. The hybrid QLSTM gives the lowest reconstruction error, with an RMSE of 0.294 GeV. Under matched-data conditions with only 1,500 training events, the QLSTM improves on the classical LSTM by 52.7% and also surpasses an LSTM trained on the full set. These observations indicate that longitudinal representations are central to calorimeter energy reconstruction and give preliminary evidence that hybrid quantum-classical sequence models can be more sample-efficient for regression in high-energy physics.


### [237] [Curvature-Aware Edge Dynamic GNNs for Low-$p_T$ Track Reconstruction at HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17343)
*Syed Haider Ali, Nouhaila Innan, Muhammad Shafique*

Main category: physics.ins-det

Abstract: Particle track reconstruction in high-energy collider experiments is a computationally demanding combinatorial problem, traditionally solved by Kalman filters that scale poorly with increasing detector occupancy. Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) have recently emerged as a promising alternative. In this work, we introduce the Manifold Helical-IN, a novel Interaction Network architecture that explicitly embeds detector spacepoints onto a helical manifold chart using curvature-aware features. This geometric projection mathematically unrolls the tightly curling trajectories of low-momentum particles. Consequently, the network can distinguish true physical tracks from combinatorial noise with good precision. Evaluated on the TrackML challenge dataset, the proposed Manifold Helical-IN achieves a reconstruction efficiency of 0.9675 in the experimentally critical $p_T > 0.5$ GeV regime, approaching recently reported Transformer-based approaches. Crucially, the manifold embedding yields a fake track rate of 0.0037. This represents a 13-fold improvement over the baseline Helical-IN (0.0508). Furthermore, the model maintains good track coherence, as quantified by our proposed Mean Hit Recovery Fraction (MHRF) of 0.858, and achieves a highly efficient inference latency of approximately 150 ms per event on a single NVIDIA A100 GPU. These results represent a critical step toward real-time soft particle tracking for the High-Level Trigger (HLT). By operating within practical HLT latency budgets, this geometry-aware approach provides a pathway to recover soft, anomalous Beyond Standard Model signatures such as unclustered energy patterns (SUEPs), that routinely evade standard hardware filters.


### [238] [Unsupervised Anomaly Detection in Measured Neutron Flux Profiles of the SAFARI-1 Research Reactor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17351)
*Rethabile Kgolobe, Pavel M. Bokov, Simon H. Connell, Charis Harley, Bongani G. Maqabuka, Lesego E. Moloko*

Main category: physics.ins-det

Abstract: Knowledge of the neutron flux distribution is essential for safe and effective reactor utilisation. In the SAFARI-1 research reactor, a set of axial flux profiles are periodically measured by activating natural copper wires inserted along the height of the fuel or control assemblies and subsequently measuring their activity using a sodium iodide detector. Improper wire insertion can, however, result in profiles that do not accurately represent the actual neutron flux distribution. Building on previously identified potential indicators of improper wire insertion, an automated approach based on unsupervised learning was developed to detect anomalous axial flux measurements. The measured data were preprocessed using missing-value imputation, decay correction, and normalisation. High-order polynomial fitting was then applied to characterise the axial flux profiles and derive features describing their shape. The Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) algorithm was employed to group flux profiles into regular and abnormal clusters based on these features with results matching the known groups. In addition, Convolutional Autoencoders were developed and trained on flux profiles from central and peripheral core positions to identify deviations from the regular profiles through reconstruction error, achieving better performance in central assemblies than in peripheral assemblies. The developed framework is intended to provide feedback to reactor operation by identifying potential wire-insertion problems in the manual technique used for axial flux monitoring at SAFARI-1.


### [239] [Waveform-based pulse timing enhanced by self-supervised neural regression models for digital nuclear instrumentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17356)
*Pengcheng Ai, Xiangming Sun*

Main category: physics.ins-det

Abstract: With the rapid development of high-sampling-rate digital nuclear devices, their applications in nuclear facilities and related fields become more acceptable, and will possibly become widespread due to the versatility and flexibility of waveform sampling systems. When digitizers reach hundreds of megahertz or gigahertz, their lower limit in time measurement probes into the sub-nanosecond region, expediting their use in a bunch of time-critical scenarios. While conventional algorithms coupled with digital sampling techniques realize fair pulse timing in certain conditions, the potentials are still open to compact and efficient machine learning models. In this paper, we intend to boost the performance of pulse timing with a new paradigm of self-supervised learning on matched waveform pairs. The proposed method combines the mainstream self-supervised loss and the auxiliary weakly-supervised loss on the reference set, jointly improving the timing performance. We apply the method in a gamma/neutron-mixed radiation field, common in nuclear facilities. The simulation and experimental results show that, the proposed method not only outperforms conventional timing algorithms, but also surpasses neural network models based on supervised learning alone. Considering the trained model is lightweight and computationally efficient, it is promising to deploy it on edge devices, like economical microcontrollers with neural processing units.


<div id='cs.CV'></div>

# cs.CV [[Back]](#toc)

### [240] [Adversarial Tracking: Attacks on Image Sensors for Object Tracking in Autonomous Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17505)
*Han Wu, Alex Chan, Ekin Can Erkus, Tousif Rahman, Rishad Shafik*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Object tracking systems estimate the motion of surrounding vehicles by linking detected objects across video frames. In particular, Tracking-By-Detection (TBD) systems combine object detectors with motion prediction and data association to maintain object identities over time. However, neural network-based detectors are vulnerable to adversarial attacks that perturb input images, and their impact on downstream trajectory estimation is not fully characterized by detection accuracy alone. Therefore, this paper evaluates image-specific and image-agnostic attacks on YOLOv3 and YOLOv4 detectors paired with SORT, Deep SORT, Strong SORT, and OC-SORT tracking systems. The evaluation uses CARLA simulator and measures tracking quality through Multiple Object Tracking Accuracy (MOTA), Higher Order Tracking Accuracy (HOTA), and Identification F1 (IDF1). Under image-specific attacks, HOTA for YOLOv3-based trackers declines from 53.45-56.19% to 14.85-17.03%, while MOTA reaches -772.60% in the worst case. YOLOv4-based trackers exhibit greater robustness, while HOTA still declines to 25.06-26.86%. In addition, image-agnostic attacks can reduce the tracking accuracy using a single Universal Adversarial Perturbation (UAP), without generating a separate perturbation for individual frames.


### [241] [Rethinking Visual Reasoning in Text-to-Image Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17585)
*Shiye Su, Xiaohan Wang, Ludwig Schmidt, Serena Yeung-Levy*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Reward models are a core component of modern text-to-image systems, serving as the objective for inference-time selection and RL-based post-training. A key prerequisite is visual understanding: to judge prompt adherence and ground aesthetic preferences, a reward model must bind attributes, resolve relations, and count reliably. We find that widely-used reward models perform poorly on targeted visual reasoning evaluations. Even when starting from strong vision-language backbones, preference fine-tuning erodes these capabilities, and continual-learning baselines fail to mitigate a steep tradeoff between visual reasoning and preference accuracy. We propose a data-centric intervention that injects visual reasoning supervision into preference learning by flipping the pairing axis. To complement conventional human preference data (paired images under a shared prompt), we convert existing visual reasoning datasets into preference pairs that share the same image but differ in prompt, and train on the mixture. This yields a Pareto improvement in both visual understanding and preference prediction. Across inference-time best-of-n selection and RL post-training, the resulting reward model improves text-to-image generation on challenging compositional prompts (GenEval) by an average of 4.0% and 5.5% respectively, outperforming HPSv3 which was trained on 6x more preference pairs.


### [242] [Training-Free Acceleration of Multi-View 3D Transformers via Regime-Aware Attention Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17629)
*Muhammed Izzet Sezer, Sevval Belkis Dikkaya*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Multi-view 3D transformers such as VGGT reconstruct a scene from a set of unposed images in a single forward pass, predicting camera poses, depth and 3D points, but the cost of their global attention grows quadratically with the num- ber of images. Accelerators borrowed from large language models assume that each token attends to only a few oth- ers; on these models they lose accuracy or gain little speed. We study the global attention of three multi-view transform- ers on seven datasets and find that every layer plays one of three roles: it averages over the whole scene, matches points across many images, or anchors on the first image, whose coordinates the outputs use. Building on this anal- ysis, we propose Regime-AWare ATTention Approximation (RAWATTA), a training-free method that runs attention in 8- bit precision and keeps 16-bit precision only in the layers where a worst-case calibration check requires it. Its fast set- ting, RAWATTA-Fast, also approximates attention in a few averaging and anchoring layers, while matching layers and the first image keep all their keys. On a 12 GB GPU at 50 im- ages, RAWATTA runs VGGT faster than the released code, mostly through our CUDA engine, and loses at most about one point of pose accuracy (AUC@3) on any dataset; RAWATTA-Fast is slightly faster and less accurate. Both fit 200 images, where the released code runs out of memory at 100.


### [243] [Creative Product Design under Brand Image Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17762)
*Takeshi Onishi, Hiroya Makino, Takahiro Yamaguchi, Hiroyuki Sakai*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Industrial design requires creative visual exploration while satisfying constraints that are often product-specific and implicit. Existing text-to-image (T2I) approaches can generate creative designs through concept blending or multi-agent self-enhancement, but they provide limited mechanisms for ensuring that generated designs remain consistent with such constraints. We focus on brand image as a representative design constraint and propose a framework for brand-aware redesign generation. Given a product name, the framework constructs a brand image survey report that summarizes product and market information, derives a creative direction from the report, and integrates this direction into a self-enhancement loop in which generated images are critiqued and prompts are revised. We evaluate the framework on 50 redesign tasks across five product categories, comparing it with Naive T2I, IP-Adapter/IT-Blender, and unconstrained self-enhancement under the same T2I backbone. By a VLM-as-Judge protocol to measure creativity and brand identifiability, the proposed framework achieves a more favorable trade-off between the two objectives, with particularly clear gains in highly constrained categories such as automotive and motorcycle, where the plausible design space is relatively limited compared with that of other product categories. These results suggest that dynamically eliciting and textualizing brand image can guide creative product design generation under brand image constraints.


### [244] [Towards Seconds-level Video Representation: Quadratically Gated Feature Grid with Adaptive, Prediction-free Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17786)
*Tie-Yun Li, Hao-Ran Wang, Chao Wang, Zhiyong Yin, Yiming Xu, Zhenwei Cai, Li Li, Rongzhi Lin, Feng-Lei Fan*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Implicit neural video representations are notoriously slow to build, which does not cross the basic practical threshold for most industrial applications. Here, we present Q-GAS, a coordinate-queryable video representation that couples multi-resolution explicit 3D feature grids with a compact SwiGLU-style quadratic-gating (QG) decoder and an error-aware, prediction-free sampler. The explicit grids avoid hash-collision coefficient sharing at the cost of modestly higher storage yet greatly boost the expressive capacity; the QG decoder introduces low-rank local second-order interactions to improve the nonlinear feature response. During fitting, a global error map periodically refreshes a high-error pool, and each batch mixes pool samples with uniform samples, which is free from the auxiliary model to predict difficult samples, thereby further escalating the efficiency. On UVG Beauty/Bosphorus/Jockey, Q-GAS fits and quantizes under 50 s on one RTX PRO 6000, decodes at over 370 FPS with mixed precision and about 146 FPS in FP32. In our PyTorch implementation, Q-GAS trains and quantizes 5.5-7.2 times faster than the evaluated Instant-NGP baseline and 19-28 times faster than RNeRV. Under a 90 s budget, it improves PSNR/MS-SSIM/LPIPS over the evaluated NeRV-family pipelines at comparable BPP over the measured overlap. We have shared our code at this https URL for readers' free use.


### [245] [Can Large Language Models Optimize Pretrained Neural Network Weights? A Multi-Phase Empirical Evaluation of Gradient-Free Editing in YOLO26 Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17789)
*Mohamed Abdallah Salem*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Direct weight editing offers a gradient-free alternative to fine-tuning pretrained detectors, but its generalization and the value of large language model (LLM) guidance remain uncertain. We evaluate five experimental phases using YOLO26 models and MS COCO, comparing deterministic scaling, random perturbations, differential evolution, CMA-ES, structured interventions, real local-LLM proposals, failure-aware guidance, and hybrid LLM-CMA-ES against a matched random-policy control. Optimization uses native Ultralytics mAP50-95 on 1,000 COCO Val2017 images; selected candidates are assessed on a disjoint, previously examined 1,500-image subset, with COCO API AP50-95 reported separately where available. Increasing the CMA-ES budget from 8 to 250 evaluations raised development gains from +0.11 to +0.28 percentage points, while selection changes remained negative (-0.03 to -0.12 points); the 250-evaluation result uses one seed. The hybrid LLM approach showed no statistically established advantage over random-policy guidance (p = 0.612, 0.369, and 0.694 at budgets 8, 25, and 50). Five-seed YOLO26m replication yielded a COCO API AP difference of +0.004 points (95% CI: -0.026 to +0.034). Of 225 archived LLM proposals, 53 satisfy the final strict schema; early tensor-level execution cannot be fully verified. Genuine LLM-mediated weight editing is feasible, but the tested approaches did not demonstrate consistent transfer beyond the development subset or a measurable LLM advantage.


### [246] [SceneAgent: A Multi-Agent Framework for Portable Game Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17821)
*Xingyi Li, Jiahao Yang, Yiqin Zhu, Jingwen Ye, Yang Sun, Weidong Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Moving a scene generator to a new game engine requires more than exporting its final output: planning, construction tools, and stored experience may also depend on the original backend. We present SceneAgent, a multi-agent framework for reusing the construction process and its experience across backends. A canonical SceneContext maintains the evolving world, while backend adapters isolate coordinate conventions, asset bindings, and export. Structured handoffs let fresh Builder sessions query this persistent state without inheriting the full construction transcript. Across generations, human-approved traces are distilled into asset-ID-free Layout skills and semantic Content skills that can be retrieved and recombined. We introduce GameSceneBench, comprising 69 indoor and outdoor prompts. Under a common asset and evaluation protocol, SceneAgent obtains human tie-adjusted preference scores of 78.2-89.4% against five baselines. Ablations show that reusable skills and the multi-agent protocol primarily improve prompt alignment and scene completeness, with additional runtime. Deployment in a second engine preserves the shared construction protocol and skill library while replacing backend integration components. Together, these results support separating persistent scene state, task-local reasoning, and reusable experience for portable scene construction.


### [247] [V-JEPA-LM: Video-Language Models That Read Dynamics Beyond Static, Text-Aligned Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17831)
*Keuntae Kim, Dongjun Kim, Yong Suk Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: World-model video encoders such as V-JEPA learn how scenes change (direction, speed, order, state) from video alone. To answer questions, they are joined to a language model trained on captions and question-answer pairs. But each caption or question names only a few attributes of a clip, so the model learns to retrieve those attributes rather than to read the clip, and the dynamics the encoder carries go unused: on the same items, a linear probe reads speed from frozen V-JEPA features at 87% while the VLM answers at chance. We show where dynamics lives in a frozen encoder: in a subspace shared across videos for predictive encoders such as V-JEPA, but not as a shared component of frame-wise, text-aligned encoders such as SigLIP. We then build counterfactual supervision that makes the language model read it: pixel transformations turn public videos into answer-balanced variants whose answers are set by the transformation and differ only in the change. Trained in a single stage on 4.53M public samples with 32 visual tokens per frame, where open video LLMs use several stages, 9M to 172M samples and 64 to 256 tokens per frame on average, the resulting V-JEPA-LM models (1B to 8B, Qwen3 and Llama) lead every size class in average score across eleven video benchmarks; our 4B model scores 72.1, against 61.1 for the best open 4B and 64.8 for the best open 8B model. A controlled encoder x supervision study shows that the encoder decides what can be read and the supervision what is read: at a matched budget, counterfactual supervision adds 4.5 points on average and 38 on the speed questions of natural benchmarks. Code and models: this https URL .


### [248] [DySENSE: Dynamic Spatiotemporal ENergy Synthesis and Emulation with Satellite Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17878)
*Kailai Sun, Zijie Gao, Mingyi He, Can Rong, Alok Prakash, Baoshen Guo, Shenhao Wang, Jinhua Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Cities account for over two-thirds of the world's energy and more than 70% of global carbon dioxide emissions. City energy is essential for achieving the 2050 net-zero target and the UN's Sustainable Development Goals 7 and 11. Although existing studies have made progress, they are limited by data and methodological gaps. Existing studies are largely predictive for existing cities rather than generative for future cities; fine-grained, aligned monthly dynamic energy data are scarce; and existing GenAI models struggle to generate dynamic urban functional monthly energy. We present DySENSE, a joint multimodal GenAI framework that synthesizes static urban satellite imagery together with spatially aligned, dynamic monthly building energy consumption maps. We decouple long-term static urban morphology from short-term dynamic energy consumption. By conditioning on static urban land use, DySENSE can generate realistic, diverse and new static urban imagery with aligned energy maps. By conditioning on dynamic monthly and climate prompts, DySENSE can jointly generate dynamic, monthly energy consumption maps while keeping the urban morphology static. Experiments across four cities (New York, Washington, Norfolk, Busan) demonstrated that DySENSE can generate annotated high-fidelity synthetic energy data using less than 25% labelled data, boosting downstream prediction performance by 8% mIoU, and achieving strong energy dynamic consistency. Compared to existing methods, DySENSE significantly improved prediction performance (8.7% accuracy) and reduced prediction error (9%-18% CVRMSE, ASHRAE). DySENSE offers an effective foundation for energy-efficient cities, peak-load analysis, building performance simulation and dynamic demand response for city, energy, and building science. The data and code: this https URL and this https URL


### [249] [Photogrammetric 3D Model-Based Animal Behavior Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17881)
*Olaf Hellwich, Moritz Heine, Patrik Reiske, Sole Traverso, Katharina Hohlbaum, Henning Sprekeler, Christa Thöne-Reineke, Lars Lewejohann, Roman Bhuiyan*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Video-based behavior estimation is often implemented end-to-end from image sequences. We advocate a two-stage alternative: reconstruct 3D body and object geometry before classifying behavior. This makes ambiguities of the ill-posed 2D-to-3D inverse problem explicit and potentially correctable. We demonstrate the strategy with simulated interacting mice and extend it to real video.


### [250] [More Genes, Faster Inference: Efficient Large-Panel Spatial Transcriptomics Prediction from Histology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17899)
*Songming Zhang, Yuxiao Luo, Shifu Luo, Siran Chen, Shuquan Su, Jinyan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Spatial transcriptomics (ST) prediction from histology aims to infer spatially resolved gene expression profiles directly from tissue images. This is a challenging problem that is currently often addressed with complicated model architectures, additional inputs, and complicated training procedures, while remaining limited to fixed, small gene panels. This raises a natural question: Is there an efficient method for large-panel ST prediction? Here, we present GeneFlow, a simpler yet effective framework for large-scale spatial gene expression prediction that unifies flow-matching generative modeling with structured multimodal fusion. Specifically, GeneFlow first encodes both histological morphology and cross-gene interactions in spatial spots through histology and gene tokenizers. The model directly predicts spatial gene expression, rather than an intermediate velocity field, while being trained with a velocity-based flow-matching objective. We show that, even without relying on heavy generative pipelines, GeneFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple ST benchmarks, with up to 56.8% gains in 1,000-gene prediction. Extensive experiments further show that GeneFlow produces biologically meaningful spot profiles while reducing computational cost by orders of magnitude, thereby improving efficiency and scalability, particularly in clinical settings. We believe GeneFlow provides an opportunity for leveraging widely available histology images for downstream biological discovery. Code is available at: this https URL


### [251] [From Images to Grounded Edits: A Real-World Benchmark for Chinese Grammatical Error Correction in Handwritten Essays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17966)
*Weiru Zhang, Yuzhong Hong, Junwei Bao, Lemao Liu, Dejing Dou*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Chinese Grammatical Error Correction (CGEC) is typically formulated as a text-to-text problem. While suitable for digital texts, this formulation falls short in real-world K-12 education, where AI-assisted corrections are expected directly on essay images, which are primarily handwritten and exhibit many irregularities. To bridge this gap, we introduce Grounded Chinese Grammatical Error Correction (G-CGEC), an image-to-grounded-edit task requiring systems to predict both corrections and their positions on essay images. We propose G-CGEC-Eval, a benchmark comprising real-world images of K-12 Chinese essays with fine-grained annotations. For evaluation, we adapt IoU-based metrics from object detection and propose a more tolerant alternative based on center-distance matching. Using this benchmark, we evaluate open-source and proprietary vision-language models under a direct single-pass setting, as well as settings with auxiliary components (OCR and character grounding) coordinated via pipelining or agentic harnessing. Results show that even top-tier proprietary models perform surprisingly poorly in the single-pass setting, while pipelining and agentic harnessing yield meaningful improvements. Notably, an error-type-based multi-agent workflow achieves the best performance, establishing it as the strongest baseline for future G-CGEC research. Through ablations and fine-grained analysis, we find the task remains far from solved, with linguistic error discovery and character-level grounding as the primary bottlenecks. This underscores the need for models that better integrate visual perception with linguistic reasoning and visual grounding.


### [252] [TelePhysica: Agentic Reconstruction of Physically Interactive Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18078)
*Ruiqi Shu, Xufeng Zhan, Yuanzhi Liang, Haibin Huang, Chi Zhang, Xuelong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Direct image-to-video diffusion models entangle pixel synthesis with implicit latent motion, precluding persistent 3D mechanical equilibrium, mid-rollout intervention, and counterfactual force control. Conversely, single-pass programmatic 3D reconstruction from an uncalibrated photograph leaves monocular depth and support clearance underconstrained, causing reconstructed scenes to interpenetrate or collapse under gravity. We present TelePhysica, a closed-loop framework that reconstructs an executable, multi-regime physical world from a single photograph to drive stateful 3D interaction and trajectory-grounded video synthesis. A vision-language coding agent synthesizes separable visual meshes and declarative physical models within a two-stage verification loop that pairs pixel-aligned silhouette critique with a zero-force gravitational equilibrium preflight. The verified world compiles into a unified solver coupling rigid bodies, articulated chains, co-dimensional cloth shells, and 3D tetrahedral elastodynamics, whose simulated rollouts condition pretrained video diffusion models through pixel-aligned structural streams. On a 100-scene single-image benchmark, closed-loop execution raises equilibrium-verified world recovery from $0\%$ in single-pass synthesis to $81\%$. Controlled constitutive ablations show over an order-of-magnitude larger localized corner displacement and out-of-plane pleating in finite-element cloth than in matched rigid-plate proxies. Conditioning video diffusion on simulated trajectories increases dynamic motion-mask overlap by $2{\times}\text{--}5{\times}$ and preserves motion direction under counterfactual force reversals where direct image-to-video models fail.


### [253] [AG-JEPA: Anatomically Guided Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture for Neurodegenerative Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18097)
*Azad Singh, Ayantika Das, Amog Rao, Aiman Farooq, Anirban Porya, Ananya Shukla, Sowbhagya Suresh, Srinivasan Iyengar, Mohit Jain, Mercy Prasanna Ranjit*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Brain MRI can reveal early signs of Alzheimers disease (AD) before measurable cognitive decline becomes apparent. However, during the transition from cognitively normal (CN) to mild cognitive impairment (MCI), the disease-related signals are often subtle and confined to only a few brain regions. Existing Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) models learn general structural representations but do not explicitly capture the anatomical regions affected in AD. We propose Anatomically Guided JEPA (AG-JEPA), which introduces anatomical tokens that interact with patch tokens during pretraining to learn region-specific representations from brain regions relevant to neurodegeneration. Building on this representation space, we further propose Prog-AG-JEPA, which models disease progression trajectory as a conditioned latent displacement within this space. We evaluate AG-JEPA across five downstream tasks spanning Alzheimer's disease diagnosis and progression prediction. AG-JEPA improves diagnosis and MCI prognosis, achieving an 8.4 percentage point AUROC gain over the strongest baseline on CN/MCI classification and a 7.1 percentage point gain on out-of-distribution CN/AD classification on the MR-RATE dataset, highlighting its effectiveness across both early-stage detection and cross-cohort generalization. For disease progression modeling, Prog-AG-JEPA consistently outperforms all baselines on future diagnosis and brain volume change prediction, demonstrating the value of anatomically grounded neurodegeneration representations for diagnosis and prognosis.


### [254] [EcoVGGT: Pinned-Key Inference for Efficient and Streamable Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction from Long Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18099)
*Hongsen Cao, Shanxin Yuan, Mona Jaber, Ahmed Sayed*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Feed-forward multi-view transformers such as VGGT recover the cameras and geometry of a video in one pass, but the cost of their cross-frame attention grows quadratically with the frame count, and their accuracy drops on long videos. Most remedies approximate attention over all frames within a budget or select a few keys by their content. We hypothesize that the keys of a long video can be chosen without looking at its content, and our experiments on VGGT and $\pi^3$ support it: a few dozen evenly spread frames can serve as the keys of every frame. We present EcoVGGT, a training-free inference framework whose keys form a Pinned Key Set of 31 such frames. A pin pass stores their tokens in a Scene Cache, and the other frames read it in independent batches that reproduce joint inference with these keys exactly, at a constant cost per frame. An Online Stream updates the pinned frames during recording. Six middle global blocks align the frames and form a reusable VGGT module: the Alignment Score reads their attention to flag failures, and Alignment Refocusing reduces them by fine-tuning only these blocks (3.6 GPU hours). EcoVGGT ($\pi^3$) matches the success rate of $\pi^3$ on 1,000-frame ScanNet videos at 13.8 times its speed. With refocusing, EcoVGGT (VGGT) raises the success rate of VGGT from 70% to 82% at 8.5 times its speed on 500 frames. EcoVGGT reconstructs 4,000 frames on one GPU, and EcoVGGT ($\pi^3$) streams 1,000 frames at 18.6 frames per second with the lowest online absolute trajectory error.


### [255] [AI-Based Multi-View Echocardiographic Image Synthesis using Dynamic Shape Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18375)
*João Freitas, Anca Balinisteanu, Ahmed Youssef, Vitor Hugo Pereira, Jan D'hooge, Sandro Queirós*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Although automated methods have improved echocardiographic workflows, progress remains constrained by the need for large, well-annotated datasets. As the development of clinical databases is often limited by ethical and logistical challenges, synthetic data generation has emerged as a promising alternative. However, existing approaches commonly rely on left ventricle shape-based supervision or are tailored to specific cardiac views, limiting the generation of additional standard and unconventional imaging planes and the preservation of multi-view consistency across cardiac chambers. To address these limitations, we propose a novel framework for shape-controlled synthesis of multi-view echocardiographic sequences. First, multi-view cross-sections are extracted from dynamic whole-heart meshes. Ultrasound-like appearance is then introduced through a two-stage AI-based pipeline. In Stage 1, an unpaired GAN-diffusion model translates these cross-sections into realistic echocardiographic frames. As this translation is performed independently at the frame level, Stage 2 employs masked video reconstruction to improve temporal coherence and cardiac motion. Evaluation demonstrated high frame-level realism for both stages, while the integration of Stage 2 substantially improved video-level motion quality and temporal consistency. Overall, the proposed framework enables the generation of realistic multi-view echocardiographic sequences from diverse cardiac models, providing a scalable source of annotated data for dataset augmentation and the development of multi-view cardiac assessment methods.


### [256] [CoPre-MoE: Consolidating and Preserving MoE for Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18417)
*Tao Hu, Ling-Xiang Zhang, Da-Wei Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Multimodal continual instruction tuning requires multimodal large language models to sequentially acquire new capabilities without catastrophic forgetting. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) architectures offer a promising way to mitigate task interference through expert specialization and combination. However, while existing MoE-based methods avoid overwriting shared parameters by allocating separate experts for each task, this progressive expansion incurs severe cross-task redundancy and linear parameter growth. Specifically, experts across tasks often learn overlapping functions alongside task-specific capabilities. For instance, ScienceQA and TextVQA both locate relevant image regions. To curb parameter redundancy without inducing forgetting, we propose Consolidating and Preserving MoE (CoPre-MoE). For each incoming task, CoPre-MoE first learns task-specific MoE to capture specialized capabilities. To eliminate redundancy and reduce parameter growth across tasks, we design a shared-and-private expert architecture. Specifically, we first evaluate functional overlap to selectively consolidate overlapping experts across tasks into a fixed-capacity generic expert pool. We then factorize the remaining task-specific experts into shared projection bases and compact task-specific cores within a private expert pool. To mitigate forgetting, we construct the router by pairing each expert with learnable retrieval keys, where historical keys remain frozen during continual updates while newly added keys are regularized with an orthogonality penalty. At inference, input-dependent routing dynamically combines both pools without test-time task labels. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CoPre-MoE achieves superior performance while substantially reducing parameter overhead.


### [257] [Provable Cross-Modal Transfer of Hierarchical Concept Structure in CLIP Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17671)
*Nghia Nguyen, Tianjiao Ding, Lachlan Ewen MacDonald, René Vidal*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Contrastive vision-language models such as CLIP are trained to align images and text, not to represent taxonomies. Yet empirical studies show that CLIP embeddings exhibit hierarchical structure aligned with the hierarchy of semantic concepts. A theoretical characterization of why such hierarchy emerges from a non-hierarchical objective remains open. In this paper, we formalize this phenomenon as Hierarchical Angular Separation (HAS), a geometric property whereby each non-leaf concept embedding is more similar to its descendants than to off-branch concepts. Empirically, we show that HAS holds approximately in raw images and randomly initialized CLIP image embeddings, while in text embeddings it holds approximately only after contrastive learning. To theoretically explain this asymmetry, we analyze an unconstrained features model in which the image embeddings are fixed and only the text embeddings are trained. At the global minimizer, the text-side HAS violation percentage at each node is at most the image-side HAS violation and near-violation percentage. The key mechanism is the hierarchical co-occurrence structure of image-text pairs, which biases text embeddings toward root-centered image prototypes. For a linear contrastive loss, we derive a closed-form solution that makes this mechanism explicit. We extend the analysis to the InfoNCE loss and establish analogous guarantees under sufficient regularization. Synthetic experiments validate the theory and show that HAS is transferred even beyond the regimes covered by our analysis.


### [258] [RaFL: An Empirical Study of Random Filter Losses for Image Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17707)
*Ruibo Wang, Fengbei Liu, Aleksei Tiulpin, Mert R. Sabuncu, Heejong Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Pixel losses train image reconstruction networks toward a point statistic of the target distribution, often averaging away fine textures under uncertainty. Perceptual losses alleviate this issue using pretrained features, but those features are inherently domain-specific. We study a random filter loss (RaFL) that compares prediction and target through filters with random weights, avoiding both pretrained features and task-specific loss engineering. RaFL combines random convolutions, random Fourier features, and random wavelet filters, automatically balancing their contributions through gradient-norm matching, eliminating manual loss-weight tuning and grid search. We systematically investigate three design choices: filter family, weight resampling schedule, and weight initialization. Based on validation PSNR and stability across random seeds, we select the configuration that uses all three filter families, redraws filters every optimization step, and employs Gaussian initialization. We evaluate RaFL on three 2D and two 3D image reconstruction tasks. RaFL outperforms a pixel loss on three tasks and matches it on two, and demonstrates stable performance across random seeds on all five tasks. RaFL adds less than 2% to the backbone's forward FLOPs and no inference cost, requiring $182\times$ fewer loss FLOPs than a VGG19 perceptual loss. These findings suggest that RaFL is a simple, tuning-free, and domain-agnostic loss objective for 2D and 3D image reconstruction.


### [259] [Efficient Genotype Classification from 3D MRI in ADPKD via Density-Aware Point Sampling and E(3)-invariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17758)
*Kwanghee Lee, Hyeongsub Lee, Youngwoo Kim, Hyunji Jeong*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Early detection of genetic kidney diseases, especially autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease (ADPKD), is crucial because certain genotypes such as truncating PKD1 mutations cause rapid kidney growth and early progression to end-stage kidney disease. MRI-based genotype inference is a potential low-cost complement to genetic testing. However, although many deep learning methods have been developed for classifying various MR images, applying them to ADPKD remains challenging: (1) abdominal MR images are generally too large to be handled by deep learning models designed for smaller images like brain MRI, and (2) the amount of labeled data for ADPKD genetic mutations is insufficient to train deep learning models. In order to address these challenges, we propose E3PN, an efficient 3D MRI-based ADPKD genotype classification network that relies on point sampling and E(3)-invariance. We utilize a point representation of 3D MRI, which consumes less GPU memory than a voxel representation. For further memory efficiency, 3D points are sampled from a mixture distribution over the two kidneys in each image. Since E(3)-invariance has been shown to improve data efficiency, we also incorporate it into our model through coordinate canonicalization. On our ADPKD dataset, E3PN trains 163 times faster and uses 103x less GPU memory than a 3D ResNet, while also improving F1 from 0.165 to 0.226 over both baselines. These results establish a first benchmark for five-way ADPKD genotype classification from 3D MRI and show that point sampling and coordinate canonicalization yield two-orders-of-magnitude efficiency gains with a modest but consistent improvement in F1.


### [260] [Learning Anisotropic Prediction Targets for Flow Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17767)
*Rayhan Zirvi, Hansheng Chen, Gordon Wetzstein, Leonidas Guibas*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Flow-based generative models are trained on a hand-chosen prediction target, such as the velocity, the noise, or the clean data. This choice strongly affects training and sample quality. We introduce Anisotropic Flow Models (AnisoFlow), which replaces a fixed prediction target with a fully learnable one. AnisoFlow learns to scale the noise component of the target anisotropically, assigning a separate nonnegative weight to each direction in a jointly learned basis. A closed-form map recovers the velocity from the network's prediction, leaving the architecture, flow-matching loss, and sampler unchanged. Since recovery is exact, training optimizes the target purely to make it easier for the network to predict. We theoretically analyze the resulting prediction error and predict that the preferred noise weight in each direction scales near-linearly with data variance, in agreement with the weights learned empirically in pixel space. AnisoFlow improves FID from 1.90 to 1.55 over the JiT baseline on ImageNet 256$\times$256, by learning only the prediction target, without representation alignment or architectural changes. In pixel-space text-to-image generation, AnisoFlow improves image quality and, through better-conditioned velocity recovery, largely removes patch-seam artifacts under reduced-precision inference.


### [261] [Domain Adaptation Based Pipeline for Character Classification and Handwritten Text Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17952)
*Florent Imbert, Simon Corbillé, Hui Han, Elisa H. Barney Smith*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Character classification and handwritten text recognition, particularly in specialized tasks, often suffer from limited labeled data, while a large amount of unlabeled data remains unused. To address this challenge, we propose a novel unsupervised domain adaptation-based pipeline to reduce the distribution gap between source and target domains. Our pipeline follows three main steps. In the first step, we pretrained an expert model on source data, to learn a strong general representation. In the second step, we perform unsupervised domain adaptation to leverage unlabeled target data, adapting the model to the specific characteristics of the target domain. In the third step, we trained the expert model on source data in the target style. By aligning the feature distributions between the source and target, our method enables the model to generalize effectively across diverse datasets. The unsupervised training increases the computation time only during training not in inference. We evaluate the pipeline on both character-level and historical handwritten line recognition tasks, demonstrating its flexibility. We establish a new state-of-the-art on the Safran-MNIST-DLS dataset defined in Track 2 of the DAGECC competition with 76.17% Macro F1-score and achieve competitive results on each of the specific datasets of READ 2018 without using the labels of the target data. Code is available at this https URL


### [262] [JaRS: Contrast-Free Localization of Memorized Regions in Diffusion Models via Jacobian Row Sums](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18024)
*Saisuchith Mahajan, Aditey Nandan, Rohit Singh, Nishanth Shetty, Chandra Sekhar Seelamantula*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Diffusion models sometimes memorize and reproduce regions of their training images, raising privacy and copyright concerns. We aim to localize these regions. Existing contrast-based methods use the diagonal of the denoiser Jacobian, which reflects the posterior variance of each pixel. However, a small diagonal entry is ambiguous: it signals either memorization or averaging over many neighboring pixels in smooth or predictable regions. To resolve this ambiguity, these methods subtract a reference map, which requires a second evaluation with a null prompt or an earlier checkpoint. Unconditional models have no null prompt, and earlier checkpoints are unavailable in general. We show that Jacobian row sums (JaRS), unlike the diagonal, separate memorization from local averaging without a reference map. Pairing JaRS with an image-level detection score that uses the same statistic at an early denoising step yields JaRS+. Across Stable Diffusion v1.4 and v2.1, Realistic Vision v5.1, and an unconditional diffusion Transformer (DiT), JaRS+ outperforms contrast-based localization methods.


### [263] [Adaptable Watermarking: Transforming Image Watermarking Models via Lightweight Adapters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18363)
*Mehrdad Saberi, Vishal Asnani, Shruti Agarwal, Soheil Feizi, John Collomosse*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: We explore the adaptability of neural image watermarking models; the extent to which one watermarking method can be transformed into another through lightweight adaptation. We introduce a framework that enables encoder-decoder watermarking architectures to become modular and upgradable, supporting multiple watermarking styles without re-training full models. Adaptation is performed by attaching a compact parameter-efficient adapter and fine-tuning under a multi-domain residual alignment loss (spatial, gradient, and frequency) while preserving bit accuracy via a secret-loss term. We evaluate this approach across a suite of recent watermarking models, including TrustMark, InvisMark, RoSteALS, HiDDeN, and WAM, demonstrating that adapted encoders closely reproduce target residuals and that adapted decoders recover messages with near-optimal accuracy under standard augmentations and distortions. We show that cross-model adaptation, with applications in interoperable and upgradable watermarking, can be achieved with as few as 1-2M trainable parameters per component (e.g., the 2M decoder adapter used in our multi-decoder inference pipeline).


### [264] [StreamEmbed: Real-Time Indexing from Pixels for Screenshot Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18516)
*Dayoon Ko, Yichuan Wang, Soyeong Jeong, Joseph E. Gonzalez, Gunhee Kim, Sewon Min*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Screen history can serve as searchable visual memory for people and agents, but unlike an offline corpus, it must be encoded and indexed as each screenshot arrives. OCR-based indexing is fast but loses visual information; visual encoding takes over two seconds. Yet screens change incrementally: most pixels repeat within and across screenshots. This raises a question: do visual encoders need to recompute their representations from scratch for every new screenshot? We present StreamEmbed, a system for incremental visual embedding that exploits this redundancy. Guided solely by pixel differences, it combines spatial token pruning, which skips blocks that repeat a neighbor, with temporal KV reuse, which reuses cached keys and values for blocks unchanged across screenshots. A small learned adapter compensates for pruning and reuse to preserve fidelity to full-screenshot embeddings. On ScreenMemEval, our benchmark of 10,426 questions over 184 hours of screen histories from two humans and a computer-use agent, StreamEmbed sets a new accuracy-latency Pareto frontier on an M2 Pro GPU: it encodes up to 3.7x faster than full-screenshot encoding without sacrificing accuracy and improves accuracy over OCR-based indexing by up to 6.3 Recall@10 points at matched latency. A memory-efficient vector index stores a simulated year of screenshots in 0.13 GB of RAM. Together, these results make real-time indexing of screen history in its native visual form practical, laying a foundation for fully local visual assistants.


### [265] [Understanding Contextual Bias in Agricultural CNNs Through Hidden-Neuron Concept Induction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18530)
*Abhilekha Dalal, Avishek Das, Samatha Ereshi Akkamahadevi*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) can classify images using background-dominated patches that contain little or none of the target object, showing that they exploit contextual information. Previous studies detected this behavior through classification accuracy but say little about what CNN hidden layers represent. We investigate whether Concept Induction can reveal interpretable visual properties associated with hidden-neuron activations on contextual image patches. Using five agricultural datasets with high, moderate, and low contextual bias, we analyze four CNN architectures at three network depths and five patch locations. Quantitative image descriptors of each patch are encoded in a knowledge graph and used as background knowledge for Concept Induction, which derives logical expressions characterizing neuron activation. These expressions are tested on held-out images using the Mann-Whitney U test and Cliff's delta. Of 48,013 tested neuron-feature pairs, 13,164 (27.4%) were validated. Validation was most frequent at intermediate depths (mean 40.2% at mid layers versus 12.0% at deep layers), and no patch location dominated across datasets. Among the tested descriptors, edge, Gabor, and Tamura texture features had the highest validation rates. Stronger contextual bias did not correspond to higher neuron-feature validation rates: moderately biased Sugarcane had the highest rate (40.5%) and highly biased Cassava the lowest (19.2%). These results suggest that behavioral context dependence and the semantic interpretability of hidden neurons capture different aspects of CNN behavior, and that Concept Induction can complement patch-based bias detection.


### [266] [BAL: Bidirectional Autoregression in Latent Space for Learning Human Movement Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18559)
*Genki Kinoshita, Ko Nishino*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Learning semantically meaningful human movement representations from 3D pose sequences is essential for human behavior modeling. Existing self-supervised methods derive supervision from artificial assumptions, e.g., invariance to augmentation within a certain range or recoverability of masked regions at a certain temporal scale, introducing heuristic hyperparameters that make the learned semantics sensitive to their settings. We propose BAL, a self-supervised framework that instead exploits supervision intrinsic to human movement: the natural ordering structure of everyday behavior. BAL autoregressively predicts in a semantically abstract latent space, analogous to how humans anticipate others' actions rather than their precise joint configurations. Bidirectional autoregressive prediction, both forward and backward in time, fully leverages these ordering constraints while enabling representation extraction using both past and future context at inference. To suppress representation collapse, BAL decomposes each prediction target into a global component capturing sequence-level semantics and a local component capturing context-independent movement. We prove that the local targets retain nontrivial angular diversity unless the representations are completely collapsed, preserving a meaningful training signal. Experiments on large-scale datasets demonstrate that BAL's representations are highly effective for motion captioning, forecasting, and interpolation, outperforming baselines on most evaluation metrics.


<div id='math.OC'></div>

# math.OC [[Back]](#toc)

### [267] [Convergence Properties of PF-Exec: Homogeneous Structure, Anchored Closed Form, and Gain Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17488)
*Hanzhang Jia, Liheng Zeng, Yiran Li, Yi Gao, Bo Ma*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: This paper establishes a layered mathematical char acterisation of PF-Exec, the algorithm of the companion system paper. Demand-proportional bandwidth allocation yields an exact first-order homogeneous decomposition, from which the equal-load bandwidth target and its feasibility bound follow in closed form. The logarithmic knee view preserves strict load order and shares its global optimum with the Jain fairness index, while the demand-weighted centroid minimises the position potential. The damped softmax association block possesses a fixed point on the product of simplices. The truncated one-dimensional gain iteration admits a complete characterisation, with geomet ric convergence below the critical gain, initial-value-dependent bounded oscillation at it, and a family of bounded two-state cycles above it. A global invariant error ball and an explicit tracking recursion complete the execution guarantee. A numerical audit confirms the structural identities on every measured state and quantifies the local amplification of the coupled association and resource dynamics. Together, the results explain how closed form recovery directions and bounded adaptive gains provide stable execution without requiring a global Euclidean contraction assumption.


### [268] [Agent-written heuristics versus commercial solvers on unsolved job-shop benchmark instances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17682)
*Andre Albrecht, Enzo Morosini Frazzon, Leandro C. Coelho, Bruno Vieira*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: Commercial solvers are the standard tool for difficult scheduling problems. We study whether a frontier large language model, used as a coding agent with open-source tools only, can match them in quality and speed. We report every run, and independent checkers verify every schedule. On the 100,000-operation job shop tai_100_1000_1, an agent-written solver reached 525,515, below OptalCP, Hexaly, and CP Optimizer. Unchanged, it improved all ten instances of the family and closed two. With equal budgets on the same workstation, it ended 0.0-1.8% above the lower bound, against 2.6-5.8% for CP Optimizer and 2.4-4.4% for Hexaly. On the Behnke-Geiger flexible job shops, an agent-written tabu search improved 11 of 32 open instances. With two transport robots, an agent-written solver improved seven of ten MKT instances. New lower bounds show that four published MKT values cannot be attained on the distributed instance files. Agent-run improvement campaigns closed eight further instances.


### [269] [The Polyak-Łojasiewicz Paper: A 10-Year Retrospective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17745)
*Hamed Karimi, Julie Nutini, Mark Schmidt*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: In 2016, we wrote a paper introducing the phrase "Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) condition" for an old idea, unsure of how readers would react. The paper ultimately had a larger impact than we expected, and we are surprised and honoured to receive the 2026 PKDD ECML Test of Time Award. With 10 years of hindsight, this note offers an informal and candid retrospective on the PL condition. We take a look back at the origins of the paper, give an AI-assisted refined version of the main theorem, review criticisms of the work, and discuss a variety of related ideas and extensions.


### [270] [Muon Attains $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ Iteration Complexity under Shuffling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18124)
*Kun Huang, Shi Pu, Karl Henrik Johansson*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: Muon is a stochastic first-order optimizer for matrix-valued parameters. It maintains an exponential moving average of stochastic gradients in a momentum buffer with coefficient $\beta$, and orthogonalizes the buffer to obtain the update direction. Muon has been reported to accelerate and stabilize the training of large language models. In practice, it is often used with shuffling (sampling without replacement), where each epoch visits the components of a finite-sum objective in a permuted order. However, its iteration complexity in this setting remains open. We analyze Muon under shuffling, with the momentum buffer orthogonalized by the polar map. For smooth nonconvex finite-sum problems, we establish an $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ iteration complexity for finding an $\varepsilon$-stationary point. This guarantee holds for every sequence of permutations, covering random reshuffling, shuffle once, and the incremental gradient method. This complexity matches that of stochastic gradient descent under shuffling in its dependence on $\varepsilon$, and improves on Muon's $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-4})$ complexity under sampling with replacement. Our guarantee requires a problem-dependent $\beta$ that approaches one as the number of components grows. In contrast, no fixed $\beta$ guarantees convergence to stationarity for every number of components. We show this by constructing a family of smooth convex problems on which, for any fixed $\beta\in[0,1)$, Muon remains bounded away from stationarity once the number of components is sufficiently large. This failure occurs for every sequence of permutations and persists even with small stepsizes. These results suggest two practical tuning rules: if the training loss stops decreasing even after reducing the stepsize, increase $\beta$; when increasing $\beta$, reduce the stepsize accordingly.


### [271] [Online Switching Control as Learning with Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18352)
*Masataka Nakamura, Kaito Ito, Hideaki Ishii*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: This paper addresses the problem of online switching control for discrete-time nonlinear systems where a controller is sequentially selected from a finite set of candidates. The goal is to generate an adaptive sequence of controllers to minimize the cumulative cost under unknown disturbances and cost functions. Specifically, we consider a full-information feedback setting where realized disturbance effects and cost functions are available after each selection. Using counterfactual costs computed from historical data, we reduce the online switching control problem to the experts problem with switching costs. Then, we propose an adaptive switching algorithm based on the Hedge algorithm with a switching suppression mechanism. Notably, the proposed algorithm achieves an expected regret of $O(\sqrt{T\log N})$, where $T$ is the time horizon and $N$ is the number of candidate controllers, while ensuring uniform boundedness of the state. This matches the optimal regret order for the standard experts problem.


<div id='stat.ML'></div>

# stat.ML [[Back]](#toc)

### [272] [Enumerating Posterior-Supported Priors: First-Order Error of Finite-Integral Cross-Categorical Measurement Morphisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17390)
*Ning Ni, Yongxin Zhu*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: In generative modeling, standard measure theory adopts the a.e. convention, which legitimately ignores any error component carried by a null-support set: a single null element can hide out-of-domain error inside null support and never be explicitly accounted for. The contribution of this paper is not a new distributional distance, but rather a verifiable structure that writes down which underlying predicate supports a high-level object, when it is invertible, and at which layer the error resides, from which we propose the enumerating posterior-supported prior framework. The framework operates on a three-layer categorical chain Quot->MorTop->Set: the first two layers posit no point elements and take binary-predicate quotient partitions as objects; the replica-index counting measure successively discriminates one-to-many collections into injections, lifting object collection from surjection to a quotient-layer bijection; the mutual exclusion of preimages in the shared measure space is resolved by an invertible MPT. Four results consolidate into Thm. 7: binary quotient-partition meta-categorical objects support the set categorical functional along categorical support via measure descent--recovery, enumerating the morphism itself rather than its preimage and object; their multiplicity is normalized through replica-index injection and accounted as an explicitly first-order error. The contribution of this paper is a transformation from nontrivial-input construction -> engineering-computable first-order output: the binary quotient-partition meta-categorical objects, supported via categorical support measure descent--recovery, constitute a substantive nontrivial input---precisely because they lift the out-of-domain error that hid inside null support into a first-order falsifiable coefficient mu_err, a finitely obtainable finite integral first-order error.


### [273] [Multifidelity Operator Inference for Dynamical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17503)
*Vignesh Sella, Anirban Chaudhuri, Karen Willcox*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: This paper presents a multifidelity (MF) formulation of Operator Inference (OpInf) for learning non-intrusive, physics-based reduced-order models (ROMs) of nonlinear dynamical systems. OpInf learns reduced operators from projections of snapshot data by solving a least squares regression problem. However, generating high-fidelity snapshots can be computationally expensive, limiting parameter coverage and the temporal extent of training trajectories for many aerospace applications, which can lead to inaccurate or unstable ROM predictions. This work proposes MF-OpInf, which combines limited high-fidelity snapshots with more plentiful low-fidelity snapshots through a control-variates-inspired MF least squares formulation to learn a single set of reduced operators across multiple trajectories. Low-fidelity trajectories provide additional parameter coverage and extend beyond the high-fidelity training time horizon. The low-fidelity snapshots are also used to construct an improved reduced basis through weighted proper orthogonal decomposition. We demonstrate MF-OpInf on a nonlinear reaction-diffusion problem with parametrized sinusoidal forcing and a two-dimensional incompressible flow past a cylinder with a parametrized inlet pulse. In these experiments, under an equal training simulation budget, MF-OpInf reduces the mean relative prediction error at unseen parameters by up to $87\%$ for the reaction-diffusion problem and $28\%$ for the cylinder flow problem over the test time regime, which extends beyond the high-fidelity training time horizon. These results demonstrate that MF-OpInf can improve temporal extrapolation and prediction at unseen parameters without increasing the training simulation budget.


### [274] [Uncertainty-Aware Continual Learning for 2D XRD Interpretation Using Simulated and Experimental Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17547)
*Zihan Wang, Hyun Sang Park, Ali Rachidi, David Elbert, Todd C. Hufnagel, Wei Chen*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Autonomous experimentation systems require characterization models that can interpret heterogeneous measurements and maintain predictive performance as data accumulate. Developing such models involves addressing domain mismatch between data sources, overconfident predictions on unfamiliar inputs, and catastrophic forgetting during sequential updates. In this work, we present a proof-of-concept framework for uncertainty-aware continual interpretation of two-dimensional X-ray diffraction (XRD) data. The framework integrates established components: maximum mean discrepancy-based domain adaptation to reduce simulation-to-experiment discrepancies, a convolutional neural network with a spectral-normalized neural Gaussian process (CNN--SNGP) for predictive uncertainty estimation, and replay with knowledge distillation for sequential model updating. We evaluate the framework using simulated and previously-collected experimental data for crystal structure classification of single-phase body-centered cubic, face-centered cubic, and hexagonal close-packed patterns. Data are organized into predefined sequential blocks to assess adaptation and retention of previously learned knowledge. Within this setting, the results show accurate classification across simulated and experimental data, improved alignment between the two domains, and reduced forgetting compared with naïve fine-tuning. Predictive uncertainty is also evaluated on unfamiliar and noisy inputs. The contribution is the integration and evaluation of these capabilities for sequential XRD interpretation. The framework provides a basis for future integration into autonomous experimentation systems, with experimental planning, instrument control, and closed-loop deployment remaining areas for future development.


### [275] [Elliptic Sampling: Optimal Interpolant Schedules for Gaussians and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18542)
*Cole Becker, Taylan Kargin, Kimia Hassibi, Asuman Ozdaglar, Pablo A. Parrilo*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Generative modeling based on stochastic interpolants requires specifying two key choices: an interpolant schedule bridging the initial and target distributions, and an efficient discretization of the resulting probability-flow ODE. In this work, we introduce a new framework for joint schedule/discretization design based on the analyticity properties of the (complexified) ODE trajectories, which in turn ensure good numerical behavior. Our formulation can be solved exactly via conformal mapping, yielding a natural family of variance-preserving elliptic schedules, parametrized by Jacobi elliptic functions. Pairing an elliptic schedule with a tailored discretization, we obtain Zoloflow, a new sampling algorithm closely connected to Zolotarev's optimal rational function approximants. Crucially, both the elliptic schedule and Zoloflow explicitly incorporate the condition number $\kappa$ of the target distribution and reduce to the classical trig schedule in the limit $\kappa \rightarrow 1$. For Gaussian targets, Zoloflow achieves minimax optimality among all oblivious score-span algorithms, attaining total-variation error $\varepsilon$ using $\Theta(\log\kappa\,\log(\sqrt d/\varepsilon))$ (exact) score queries. For the log-concave case, the elliptic schedule allows sampling with at most $\varepsilon$ 2-Wasserstein-error by requiring merely $\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{d} \, \mathrm{polylog}(\kappa, 1/\varepsilon))$ score evaluations. Complementing these theoretical results, we empirically evaluate the elliptic schedule on general (non-log-concave) target distributions, including detailed comparisons with alternative schedule proposals on both synthetic benchmarks and existing image generation datasets.


### [276] [Efficient and Practical Clustering of Variables in Causal Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17971)
*Otto Tabell, Jani Nykänen, Santtu Tikka, Juha Karvanen*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Clustering variables in a graph may improve visual clarity and simplify causal inference. Harnessing the full potential of clustering requires an automated method to detect useful clusters in a graph. We introduce a novel class of clusters called conduits, and present a sound, complete and efficient algorithm for detecting all conduits in the graph. In addition to algorithmic detectability, conduits appear naturally in causal graphs stemming from real-world applications, enabling substantial size reduction. Furthermore, the structure of conduits enables the application of known identifiability results regarding clustering. We demonstrate these properties in a simulation study with real-world graphs.


### [277] [Training-point consistency of kernel ridge regression with varying correlation scales](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18095)
*Weiyan Mu, Shifeng Xiong, Yuming Shen*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: The scale parameter in kernel ridge regression plays a pivotal role in modeling the correlation structure of data. In modern applications, sampling density and the underlying correlation range often change simultaneously, making a fixed-scale assumption highly restrictive. However, establishing asymptotic theories under varying scale parameters is fundamentally challenging. To address this gap, this paper develops a unified theoretical framework for kernel ridge regression under varying scale parameters. We establish that, on uniform tensor grids with product exponential kernels, the consistency of the training-point risk holds if and only if the effective local sample size diverges to infinity. This sharp criterion universally covers all regimes along arbitrary deterministic scale sequences, seamlessly unifying the long-range, fixed-scale, and short-range. Furthermore, we provide an explicit, signal-independent construction of a deterministic regularization penalty that guarantees this consistency. Numerical experiments illustrate the criterion and the corresponding penalty across all the three regimes.


### [278] [Detection and Classification in Latent Spaces: High-Dimensional Analysis and Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18164)
*Enis Chenchene, Anil S. Baslamisli, Paolo Braca*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Foundation models produce high-dimensional latent representations in which suitably designed statistical decision rules can be effective yet analytically tractable. We study such detectors in the high-dimensional regime, where latent dimension and training sample size grow proportionally. For linear and quadratic statistics, we derive explicit large-deviation characterizations of false-alarm and miss-detection performance under the Neyman-Pearson paradigm, and show how the binary analysis extends to multi-class classification. The resulting error probabilities and corresponding decay rates exhibit non-monotonic dependence on the shape parameter, including double-descent and, in some regimes, multiple-descent. Experiments on vision, text, and audio datasets using modern foundation models show close agreement between theoretical predictions and empirical performance. In particular, the proposed framework allows us to set a prescribed false-alarm level and accurately predict the corresponding miss-detection probability. Overall, our results provide a theoretically grounded and interpretable framework with competitive empirical performance across different modalities.


### [279] [LaMe: a Joint Latent Mediation Framework for High-Dimensional Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18180)
*Gaël Poux-Médard, Kaniav Kamary, Yohann De Castro, Vivian Viallon*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Mediation analysis seeks to understand how an exposure influences an outcome through intermediate variables. However, as increasingly rich datasets become available, hundreds to thousands of potential mediators make these systems difficult to model and hard to interpret. Latent-variable approaches offer a natural way to accommodate these high-dimensional settings. However, existing latent mediation methods face limitations, including overparameterization, biased inference, or limited scalability. We develop LaMe, a joint linear-Gaussian framework that summarizes a high-dimensional exposure-mediator-outcome system through a small number of latent variables. We identify natural direct and indirect effects under an explicit assumption that the manifest measurements fully capture the latent mediators, and characterize conditions under which the latent representation effectively restricts the observed cross-covariance structure. Simulations assess recovery of observed association parameters, natural effects, and manifest features associated with latent mediation pathways across a range of challenging settings. Four real-world applications demonstrate competitive predictive performance with compact latent representations, including single-dimensional representations of biomarker and proteomic measurements. Together, these results establish LaMe as a practical framework for estimating and summarizing multivariate mediation systems under explicit structural and measurement assumptions.


### [280] [Lipschitz-Constrained Discriminators in Generative Modeling: Asymptotic Behavior and Support Mismatch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18394)
*Eduardo Pinto, Panagiota Birmpa*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of optimal discriminators associated to $L-$Lipschitz regularized KL-divergences of two probability distributions $Q$ and $P$ that interpolate between the rescaled Wasserstein$-1$ metric by some parameter $L>0$ and the KL divergence. The parameter $L$ plays the role of a Lipschitz constraint and is viewed as a regularization parameter: small values of $L$ enforce a more geometry-aware discriminator, while as $L$ increases the geometric regularization is progressively relaxed. It is a known result [3] that such divergences converge to the KL divergence as $L\to\infty$. In this paper, we characterize the asymptotic behavior of the optimal discriminators as $L\to\infty$ in different regimes: Firstly, under $Q\ll P$ and the Radon-Nikodym derivative $\frac{dQ}{dP}$ is bounded from above and bounded away from zero. Secondly, when $Q$ and $P$ are discrete distributions satisfying $Q\ll P$, and finally when $Q$ and $P$ are non absolutely continuous discrete distributions. This result motivates training strategies which involve discriminators, such as adversarial generative models. In practice, the data distribution is often represented by a discrete empirical measure, while, especially at the early stages of the training, the model distribution may have little or no overlap with the empirical data distribution.


### [281] [Contrastive latent representation learning with denoising score matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18469)
*Christopher Marouani, Grigory Bartosh, Christian A. Naesseth*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Contrastive representation learning methods operate by pulling different views of the same data sample together while pushing them away from views of different samples. A fundamental challenge is the estimation of the repulsive force, which typically requires large batch sizes, can exhibit high variance, and is susceptible to the curse of dimensionality. We introduce ScoreCLR, a contrastive representation learning method that offers a novel way to estimate this repulsion term. We consider the framework of representation learning by maximizing the mutual information between two latent views of a sample. By deriving the gradient of the Shannon entropy with respect to the encoder parameters, we obtain an equivalent objective that involves the score of the latent representation. We then use denoising score matching techniques to estimate this score with a lightweight network that is trained jointly with the encoder. We further propose regularization techniques using different noise levels and a Gaussian prior on the latent representation. ScoreCLR replaces the batch of explicit negative samples in contrastive methods with a learned repulsion whose cost is linear in the batch size rather than quadratic. On CIFAR-10 and ImageNet-100 it does so with minimal loss of linear-probe accuracy compared to SimCLR under a shared recipe, while producing projector outputs of higher effective rank, closer to the Gaussian prior.


### [282] [Bregman geometry in regular exponential families: dual representations and relative smoothness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18480)
*Mame Diarra Fall, Éric Barat, Nicolas Dobigeon*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Regular exponential-family distributions are ubiquitous in statistics due to their appealing and convenient properties. Notably, they can be expressed in terms of Bregman divergences, revealing fundamental connections between statistics, convex analysis, and information geometry. However, this geometric structure has not been systematically exploited to characterize these distributions with respect to their expectation parametrization. Building on a projective characterization, we establish a general construction of equivalent dual Bregman representations, together with normalization and uniqueness results. These representations express log-densities as ordinary Bregman divergences in dual coordinate systems, providing geometric characterizations of the parameter space. So-called $\beta$- and $\alpha$-scaled dual representations emerge as particular instances of this framework. We further derive dual Bregman representations of conjugate priors and establish that they preserve conjugacy. Applications to classical distributions illustrate the geometric structures underlying seemingly different statistical models. Moreover, we show that these representations provide natural reference geometries for establishing relative smoothness of the primal Bregman representation of the distribution, with explicit bounds that are sharp in the scalar and separable settings. An illustrative application to the Poisson distribution sheds new light on several works in the literature, providing complementary geometric justifications for reference geometries while deriving existing algorithms.


<div id='physics.ao-ph'></div>

# physics.ao-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [283] [Tracing Uncertainties of Extreme El Niño Prediction to Mesoscale Ocean Currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17854)
*Bo Qin, Zeyun Yang, Qiaofei Xue, Yanan Ding, Jinyu Wang, Chaopeng Ji, Shijin Yuan, Bin Mu, Wansuo Duan, Guokun Dai, Mu Mu, Yi Deng*

Main category: physics.ao-ph

Abstract: The predictability of the El Niño-Southern Oscillation (ENSO) has historically been linked to large-scale errors in models' initial conditions such as heat content and surface wind stress. Here we demonstrate that these slowly evolving errors may ultimately originate from mesoscale errors in initial oceanic states that are common in current models and ocean observation systems. This hidden source of ENSO forecast uncertainty, in terms of mesoscale ocean-current errors, is convincingly exposed by the conditional nonlinear optimal perturbation (CNOP) method applied to a differentiable ocean AI model. The CNOP-identified most influential current errors are strongly event-dependent. For the 1997/98 El Niño, the initial current errors in the eastern Pacific results in the largest forecast error through locally suppressing original subsurface warming. For the 2015/16 event, the dominate error originates from the western Pacific, which reorganizes upper ocean temperature structure and affects the eastern Pacific through the equatorial waveguide.
