Further analysis reveals that these current errors perturb mesoscale eddy count and eddy kinetic energy triggering large-scale temperature errors that hinder El Niño development. The timing of the maximum growth rate of the obtained CNOP error is well aligned with the spring predictability barrier of ENSO, and the error structures are reflected in simulated analysis errors caused by unresolved mesoscale currents. These findings extend the potential source of ENSO predictability beyond large-scale thermal signals, reveal for the first time an uncharted route of ENSO forecast failure, and provide an important physical basis for targeted ocean observations and data assimilation in the future.


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [284] [Agentic AI for Discovering Fault-Tolerant Quantum Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18504)
*Aritra Lahiri, Florian Marquardt*

Main category: quant-ph

Abstract: Scaling quantum computing toward practical quantum advantage is currently limited by the efficiency of circuits that operate on information encoded by quantum error-correcting codes. The full stack of quantum computing encompasses several tasks: preparation of logical code states, implementation of logical gates, logical routines on encoded qubits, etc. Yet, these circuits are largely designed by hand for individual cases, with some recent works incorporating machine learning for certain aspects. We formulate this problem as agentic evolutionary program synthesis, wherein a large language model, leveraging its pretraining knowledge and tool-use proficiency, and guided by a circuit verifier, evolves a program that defines a Clifford circuit for a wide range of curated tasks such as logical state preparation, logical gate design, and logical routines such as code-switching, code growth, etc. Our approach consistently finds fault-tolerant circuits for all of these tasks, each verified by an exhaustive check over faults.


### [285] [Structure makes quantum circuits trainable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18378)
*Wenbo Xie, Wojciech Szpankowski, Ananth Grama*

Main category: quant-ph

Abstract: The scalability of variational quantum algorithms is limited by barren plateaus, where gradients vanish exponentially in system size. This worst-case result raises fundamental questions about the trainability of quantum AI models. We present a major advance towards resolving this trainability question by answering whether a given quantum circuit has a barren plateau. We do this using an analytical framework that bounds the gradient variance for arbitrary input states $\rho$ and observables $\mathcal{M}$ by decomposing the unitary representation of the associated compact Lie group. When the circuit segments on both sides of a gate form $2$-designs, we obtain the variance exactly and prove a sufficient condition for trainability: if $\rho^{\otimes 2}$ and $\mathcal{M}^{\otimes 2}$ are sufficiently concentrated on the same irreducible invariant subspace of polynomial dimension, and the differentiated gate moves this subspace by an inverse-polynomial amount, then the gradient variance is at least inverse-polynomial. We further present a sampling algorithm that estimates the gradient variance from runs of the ansatz, without constructing the representation-theoretic projectors. Our algorithm decides whether the variance exceeds a tolerance $\tau$ with the number of runs polynomial in $1/\tau$, including cases in which the bounds are inconclusive. This resolves the question of the trainability of the circuit before expensive training procedures.


<div id='cs.LO'></div>

# cs.LO [[Back]](#toc)

### [286] [Active Learning of Temporal Specifications through Distinguishing Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17866)
*Rajarshi Roy, Xiaowei Huang*

Main category: cs.LO

Abstract: Temporal specifications are essential for verification and monitoring, yet writing formulas that capture the intended behaviour is difficult. Active learning derives such specifications through interaction between a learner and a teacher, but remains underexplored for temporal logics. Existing approaches require the teacher to answer equivalence queries and to construct counterexamples, and they often rely on SAT-based learning procedures that restart the search after each new example. We present an active-learning framework for LTLf in which the teacher only labels traces and answers natural-language questions about the intended behaviour. The learner maintains an enumerated version space of candidate formulas, generates traces that distinguish candidates, and uses the teacher's hints to rank candidates or to restrict the search. We prove consistency with the observed examples, identification within bounded hypothesis classes with a bound on the number of queries, and a PAC guarantee for the final hypothesis. In experiments with a simulated teacher on 36 targets, including targets in four standard environments, the learner identifies every target when it compares all rivals, with fewer than seven queries beyond an initial sample of ten traces on average. Correct hints reduce the number of queries by up to 16% and the number of generated candidates by a fifth, and hints used for ranking never prevent identification, even when incorrect. Our enumeration-based version space is faster than the SAT-based approach of previous learners at every target size.


<div id='cs.NI'></div>

# cs.NI [[Back]](#toc)

### [287] [INC-SRv6: In-Network Route Exploration and Fast Failover Using Segment Routing Headers in Dynamic UAV Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17659)
*Zhiyuan Ren, Zhao Li*

Main category: cs.NI

Abstract: Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) networks require a network provisioning mechanism that guarantees delivery and latency quality of service (QoS) under dynamic conditions in next-generation airborne mesh networks by continuously learning and repairing multi-hop paths under rapid link turnover without flooding the control plane. This paper presents INC-SRv6, an in-network computing routing mechanism that repurposes the IPv6 Segment Routing Header (SRH) and its Segments Left (SL) pointer into a self-learning, fast-failover distributed control plane. Rather than treating the SRH as a static source-committed vector, INC-SRv6 uses the active SL pointer to partition the segment list in flight: slots ahead of SL provide capacity for hop-by-hop next-hop recording, while the suffix behind SL serves as an in-band multi-destination reachability vector. Through this dual-zone header discipline, transit routers extract distance-weighted reachability updates from arriving suffixes, gate admission by single-pass confidence with bounded binary probing, advance or reverse SL deterministically, and restore pre-transmission SRH state for local failover upon link loss. Simulations across 200-node 3D UAV networks show that under high-speed flight and repeated 20% relay outages, INC-SRv6 improves packet delivery over reactive routing (92.12% versus 89.12%) while cutting 95th-percentile tail delay from 121.26 to 8.84 ms and control packets by 35.8%, with a 4-byte compact uSID stride model reducing SRv6 control bytes by over 52%.


### [288] [PUMA-Former: Uncertainty-Aware Agentic Computation Offloading in MEO-LEO Satellite Networks With Incomplete Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18139)
*Yi Jing, Chunxiao Jiang, Jiawei Wang, Jiachen Sun*

Main category: cs.NI

Abstract: Satellite edge computing distributes mobile workloads across moving processors whose service capacity must be coordinated over time. In medium Earth orbit (MEO)--low Earth orbit (LEO) networks, periodic reports leave CPU states stale or missing, making offloading sensitive to uncertain capacity and shared-destination congestion. We propose PUMA-Former, an uncertainty-aware agentic framework for cooperative offloading under incomplete information that balances completed work, service delay, and execution cost. A MEO coordinator maintains distinct-report memory and closes the loop between available evidence, capacity estimates, allocation commands, and execution feedback. Latent-delay attention weights plausible measurement-age hypotheses, and historical innovations refine CPU estimates around the latest received report. Queue-error-aware fusion incorporates auxiliary pressure while accounting for backlog uncertainty. The inferred resource moments condition a structured Transformer allocator for joint destination, partition, bandwidth, CPU, and power decisions. Bounded risk-aware calibration then adjusts workload splits to reflect uncertainty and shared destination load. In traffic-driven development evaluation, PUMA-Former increases admitted compute-work completion by 8.62%, reduces mean modeled service delay by 34.78%, and reduces mean episode-P95 delay by 71.08% relative to ATLA-adapt. It also improves completion and normalized energy over fixed-half round-robin offloading. Service gains over the learning baselines involve higher normalized energy.


### [289] [PRISM: A Post-Prediction Reliability Framework for Evidence-Guided Correction and Selective Abstention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18127)
*Omm Swarup, Shrishail M. Hiremath*

Main category: cs.NI

Abstract: A model may produce a prediction with high confidence and still get the outcome wrong. This is a challenge in sequential applications, where the system evolves over time and deployment data may differ from the data used during training. To address this issue, we propose PRISM (Prediction Reliability and Intelligent Self-Correction Mechanism), a framework that evaluates prediction reliability after the initial output is generated. It estimates the risk of error and examines predictions identified as potentially unreliable. For these cases, PRISM analyses available signals, retrieves similar situations from historical data, and verifies whether an alternative prediction is supported by the evidence. It can then retain the original prediction, apply a verified correction, or abstain when the evidence is insufficient. We evaluate PRISM on second-innings match prediction in the Indian Premier League (IPL). Ball-by-ball records are converted into over-level observations to capture changes in match conditions throughout an innings. To preserve temporal order, earlier IPL seasons are used for training, IPL 2024 for validation, and IPL 2025-2026 for out-of-time testing. The dataset contains 8,665 over-level observations from 475 matches, with the test partition comprising 2,568 observations from 142 matches. In one experimental record, Logistic Regression, Random Forest, and HistGradientBoosting achieve accuracies of 65.2%, 68.4%, and 70.2%, respectively. The corresponding PRISM evaluation reports a selective accuracy of 82.4% at 88.5% coverage, with a Brier score of 0.126 and an expected calibration error (ECE) of 0.019.


<div id='physics.app-ph'></div>

# physics.app-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [290] [Contrastive local learning in large nonlinear analog circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17561)
*Pragyan Pandey, Peter Wu, Deema Alyahya, Eric Y. Ma*

Main category: physics.app-ph

Abstract: Machine learning in physical hardware promises to reduce training cost by colocating computation, memory and adaptation within device physics. Contrastive local learning networks (CLLNs) offer a compelling route: an electrical circuit relaxes to equilibrium in free and weakly clamped states, and each adaptive element updates from local voltage contrasts rather than backpropagated error signals. Yet whether nonlinear transistor implementations can scale beyond proof-of-concept circuits, and what design principles control that scaling, have remained unknown. Here we show that nonlinear analog circuits trained by contrastive local rules can learn larger machine-learning tasks and remain robust under hardware nonidealities. Using device-level circuit simulations, we systematically explore initialization, operating regime, loss-dependent clamping and training parameters, and scale CLLNs beyond ten thousand adaptive edges. The same local rule trains classifiers that reach the accuracy regime of conventional digital methods on Ionosphere and handwritten-digit benchmarks, and a network that autoregressively generates language over a 122-token vocabulary. These results establish the feasibility of local learning in large nonlinear analog circuits.


<div id='cond-mat.mtrl-sci'></div>

# cond-mat.mtrl-sci [[Back]](#toc)

### [291] [Graph diffusion transformer for interpretable generation and inverse design of disordered atomic structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18420)
*Jiani Hu, Chenyan Wang, Yongkun Yang, Guanyu Hou, Jihaochen Zhang, Lei Liu, Ji Chen*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

Abstract: Atomic disorder offers a potent degree of freedom to tailor material properties, yet the resulting vast configuration space obscures structure-property relations and hinders rational inverse design. Here we introduce Graph Amorphous Diffusion Transformer (GaDiT), a generative model that combines dynamic periodic graphs, masked conditioning and an interval-weighted training-noise distribution. GaDiT generates high-quality disordered structures while accelerating sampling by two orders of magnitude compared with existing corrector-based approaches, as demonstrated across two-dimensional amorphous carbon and three-dimensional amorphous silicon and silica. Moreover, GaDiT supports conditional generation under single or combined structural features, physical properties and preparation conditions in a unified model, and enables inverse design that expands accessible structure-property ranges beyond the training distribution. To interpret these structures and the trained network, we propose a conditioned atomic response mapping analysis, revealing both the global structural descriptors and the local atomic environments that govern target properties. Overall, GaDiT establishes a general framework for interpretable generation, inverse design and structure-property discovery for disordered atomic structures.


<div id='math.NA'></div>

# math.NA [[Back]](#toc)

### [292] [Score-based neural ordinary differential equations for reaction-diffusions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18467)
*Jianguo Hou, Wuchen Li*

Main category: math.NA

Abstract: A diffusion equation can be rewritten as a transport equation driven by the score function of the evolving density. Conventional probability-flow formulations, which move particles deterministically with this score-driven velocity, typically conserve total mass. In this work, we develop a Lagrangian method for reaction-diffusion equations in which each particle carries a position and a physical weight. The characteristic ODEs incorporate source terms into the evolution of the log-density and its gradient, while particle weights track changes in mass. We combine this representation with a learned velocity field and weighted particle training to approximate these dynamics. For non-increasing reaction rates, a relative-entropy inequality supports the mass-normalized self-consistency residual as the training objective. This objective can control the error of the induced density when the growth rate is bounded. The algorithm divides the time interval into short windows. In each window, a frozen velocity generates the particle data for a regression of the network velocity. We draw the particles from period-adapted initial sampling distributions and weight them in the loss partly by their predicted future mass, because the reaction can make regions with little initial mass important later. We numerically investigate the method on the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck (OU) equation with a linear source term, Fisher--KPP equations, and Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao gradient flows.


<div id='eess.IV'></div>

# eess.IV [[Back]](#toc)

### [293] [A thorough review on Deep Learning approaches to 2D-to-3D Registration in Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17468)
*Andrea Borghesi, Julia van Beurden, Maarten van Alphen, Michiel van den Brekel, Luc Karssemakers, Jan-Jakob Sonke, Baris Karakullukcu, Jonas Teuwen, George Yiasemis*

Main category: eess.IV

Abstract: 2D-to-3D image registration is a key component for several medical imaging purposes such as intraoperative guidance, enabling the alignment between preoperative 3D volumes and real-time 2D images. While conventional optimisation-based methods often struggle with non-convex objectives, sensitivity to initialisation, and high computational demands, deep learning (DL) has emerged as a promising alternative, offering improved alignment robustness and the potential for real-time performance. This review provides a comprehensive analysis of DL frameworks developed to address the challenges inherent in 2D-to-3D registration. We first outline how core concepts of DL are adapted to the registration problem. We then examine recent methodological advancements from multiple perspectives, including pipeline design, architectural innovations, and domain adaptation strategies. Particular attention is given to quantitative metrics, highlighting how performance is defined and measured across studies. Across the 41 included studies, we find that direct regression is the most common pipeline (23 studies, against 18 iterative ones), that hybrid CNN-attention architectures are gaining ground, and that research remains heavily concentrated on X-ray-to-CT registration. We conclude by identifying the absence of per-case uncertainty estimation and the strong neglect of temporal or sequential modelling as the two methodological gaps most consequential for clinical readiness, and discuss state-space models as a way to add temporal memory within intraoperative latency budgets. Further opportunities lie in more principled domain adaptation and in the integration of foundation models for representation learning.
